\mathbb{E}|X_{1}|^{s}<\infty
for some
{s}\geq 2
. Additionally, assume that
2
is satisfied and there exists
p\in[1,\infty)
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
with
0<\alpha\leq c(s)
such that
\|f_{n}\|_{L^{p}}\leq\exp(n^{\alpha})
for all
n
sufficiently large. Let
m=\lfloor{s}\rfloor
and
a_{n}=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}(1)
with polynomial order. Then

\|f_{n}-\phi^{q}_{m,n}\|_{L^{p}}\leq\sup_{|h|\leq a_{n}}\big\|f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}})-f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}}+h)\big\|_{L^{p}}+\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\,(\log n)^{({s}+2m)/2}\big).

(6.2)

Set
\beta_{n}=a_{n}^{2}\cdot n^{-\max\{2\alpha,(s-2)/2\}}
and recall
f_{n}^{\prime}
from
4.2
. Now,
Proposition

4.6
yields

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\,(1+|x|)^{m}|f_{n}^{\prime}(x)-\phi^{q}_{m,n}(x)|=r_{n}

for
r_{n}=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\,(\log n)^{({s}+2m)/2}\big)
.
By Minkowski’s inequality and
Theorem

4.1
,

\displaystyle\|f_{n}-\phi^{q}_{m,n}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\|f_{n}-f_{n}^{\prime}\|_{L^{p}}+\|f_{n}^{\prime}-\phi^{q}_{m,n}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\|f_{n}-f_{n}^{\prime}\|_{L^{p}}+\|(1+|\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}}|)^{-m}\|_{L^{p}}\cdot r_{n}

\displaystyle=\|f_{n}-f_{n}^{\prime}\|_{L^{p}}+\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\,(\log n)^{({s}+2m)/2}\big).

Next, for
b_{n}:=\sqrt{\beta_{n}}\cdot n^{\alpha}\leq a_{n}
and by
[
1
, 4.13(1)]
,

\displaystyle\|f_{n}-f_{n}^{\prime}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\big(f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}})-f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}}-y)\big)\phi_{\beta_{n}}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|y|\leq b_{n}\}}\,\mathrm{d}y\Big\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big\|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\big(f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}})-f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}}-y)\big)\phi_{\beta_{n}}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|y|>b_{n}\}}\,\mathrm{d}y\Big\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\phi_{\beta_{n}}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}})\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}}|\leq b_{n}\}}\big\|_{L^{1}}\cdot\sup_{|h|\leq b_{n}}\big\|f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}})-f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}}+h)\big\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\quad+\big\|\phi_{\beta_{n}}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}})\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}}|>b_{n}\}}\big\|_{L^{1}}\cdot\sup_{|h|>b_{n}}\big\|f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}})-f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}}+h)\big\|_{L^{p}}.

As
1-\Phi(x)\leq\phi(x)/x
, for
n
sufficiently large, the expression above is bounded by

\displaystyle\sup_{|h|\leq b_{n}}\big\|f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}})-f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}}+h)\big\|_{L^{p}}+2\big(1-\Phi\big(b_{n}/\sqrt{\beta_{n}}\,\big)\big)\cdot 2\|f_{n}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{|h|\leq b_{n}}\big\|f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}})-f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}}+h)\big\|_{L^{p}}+2\big(1-\Phi(n^{\alpha})\big)\cdot 2\|f_{n}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{|h|\leq a_{n}}\big\|f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}})-f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}}+h)\big\|_{L^{p}}+4\,n^{-\alpha}\exp(-n^{2\alpha}/2+n^{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\sup_{|h|\leq a_{n}}\big\|f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}})-f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}}+h)\big\|_{L^{p}}+\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\,(\log n)^{({s}+2m)/2}\big)

in particular.
∎

The term

\displaystyle\sup_{|h|\leq a_{n}}\|f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}})-f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}}+h)\|_{L^{p}}

from
6.2
is known as the integral (or
L^{p}
) modulus of continuity. There exists plentiful literature on bounds of this term if the Fourier transform of
f_{n}
satisfies various kinds of tail bounds (see e.g.
[
21
,
26
,
44
]
).

Next, we derive a bound on the
L^{1}
modulus of continuity of
f_{n}
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Assume that
X_{1}
is symmetric, the distribution of
X_{1}
is non-singular,
1
is satisfied and
\mathbb{E}|X_{1}|^{2m}<\infty
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
m\geq 3
. Let
a_{n}=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-(9m-17)/2}\big)
. Then there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
n\geq N
, the statistics
T_{n}
have densities
f_{n}
that satisfy

\displaystyle\sup_{|h|\leq a_{n}}\|f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}})-f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}}+h)\|_{L^{1}}=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-(m-2)/2}\big).

(6.3)

Analogously to the proof of
Theorem

4.1
,
1
and
\mathbb{E}|X_{1}|^{2m}<\infty
yield that there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
4.17
and
4.16
hold for all
n\geq N
. Throughout the remainder of the proof assume
n\geq N
. Now
4.17
implies that the self-normalized sums
T_{n}
have densities
f_{n}
. We expand

|f_{n}(x)-f_{n}(x-h)|\leq K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3}

as in
4.21
, where the terms
K_{1}
,
K_{2}
and
K_{3}
are introduced. This time, the
2m
-th moment is assumed to be finite. Therefore with
w=m
,
4.23
and
4.4
yield

K_{1}+K_{2}\leq|h|c(m)n^{4m-8},

and thus

\displaystyle|f_{n}(x)-f_{n}(x-h)|

\displaystyle\leq|h|c(m)n^{4m-8}+K_{3}

\displaystyle=|h|c(m)n^{4m-8}

\displaystyle\quad+R_{n}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(1+\|\gamma_{x,n}(z)\|\big)^{-m}\big(n^{-1/2}z\big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\big(1+\|\gamma_{x-h,n}(z)\|\big)^{-m}\big(n^{-1/2}z\big)^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}z

for all
|x|<2\sqrt{n}
and
|h|<1
. Here,
R_{n}^{\prime}=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-(m-2)/2}\big)
and
\gamma_{x,n}(z)=\big(x(n^{-1/2}z)^{1/2},\allowbreak z-n^{1/2}\big)
. By
Remark

3.10
,

\begin{split}\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle\sup_{|h|\leq a_{n}}\|f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}})-f_{n}(\raisebox{-2.15277pt}{\scalebox{2}{$\cdot$}}+h)\|_{L^{1}}$\mbox{}\hfil\quad\\
&\leq\sup_{|h|\leq a_{n}}\int_{-2\sqrt{n}}^{2\sqrt{n}}|h|c(m)n^{4m-8}\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\quad+\sup_{|h|\leq a_{n}}\int_{-2\sqrt{n}}^{2\sqrt{n}}R_{n}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(1+\|\gamma_{x,n}(z)\|\big)^{-m}\big(n^{-1/2}z\big)^{1/2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\big(1+\|\gamma_{x-h,n}(z)\|\big)^{-m}\big(n^{-1/2}z\big)^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x.\end{split}

(6.4)

Regarding the first summand,

\sup_{|h|\leq a_{n}}\int_{-2\sqrt{n}}^{2\sqrt{n}}|h|c(m)n^{4m-8}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq c(m)a_{n}\,n^{(8m-15)/2}\\
=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-(m-2)/2}\big).

(6.5)

For the second summand, it is obviously impossible to attain a bound in
h
. Therefore, we have to find a bound in
n
.
For
m\geq 3
, by Fubini’s theorem

\displaystyle\int_{-2\sqrt{n}}^{2\sqrt{n}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(1+\|\gamma_{x,n}(z)\|\big)^{-m}\big(n^{-1/2}z\big)^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-2\sqrt{n}}^{2\sqrt{n}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(1+\|\gamma_{x,n}(z)\|^{2}\big)^{-3/2}\big(n^{-1/2}z\big)^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x

(6.6)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(n^{-1/2}z\big)^{1/2}\int_{-2\sqrt{n}}^{2\sqrt{n}}\big(1+n^{-1/2}zx^{2}+(z-n^{1/2})^{2}\big)^{-3/2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z.

Applying
Lemma

A.8
gives

\begin{split}\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle\int_{-2\sqrt{n}}^{2\sqrt{n}}\big(1+n^{-1/2}zx^{2}+(z-n^{1/2})^{2}\big)^{-3/2}\,\mathrm{d}x$\mbox{}\hfil\quad\\
&=2\big(1+4n^{1/2}z+(z-n^{1/2})^{2}\big)^{-1/2}(1+(z-n^{1/2})^{2})^{-1}2n^{1/2}\end{split}

(6.7)

such that we have to examine

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(n^{-1/2}z\big)^{1/2}\big(1+4n^{1/2}z+(z-n^{1/2})^{2}\big)^{-1/2}(1+(z-n^{1/2})^{2})^{-1}n^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}z.

We split this integral into three regions that we treat separately. First,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\sqrt{n}/2}\big(n^{-1/2}z\big)^{1/2}\big(1+4n^{1/2}z+(z-n^{1/2})^{2}\big)^{-1/2}(1+(z-n^{1/2})^{2})^{-1}n^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(0)\int_{0}^{\sqrt{n}/2}1\cdot n^{-1/2}\,n^{-1}\,n^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}z\leq c(0)n^{-1/2}.

Second, by
Lemma

A.6

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{n}}^{2\sqrt{n}}\big(n^{-1/2}z\big)^{1/2}\big(1+4n^{1/2}z+(z-n^{1/2})^{2}\big)^{-1/2}(1+(z-n^{1/2})^{2})^{-1}n^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(0)\int_{\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{n}}^{2\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{2}\cdot n^{-1/2}\,(1+(z-n^{1/2})^{2})^{-1}\,n^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad=c(0)\int_{\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{n}}^{2\sqrt{n}}(1+(z-n^{1/2})^{2})^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}z\leq c(0).

Finally,

\displaystyle\int_{2\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}\big(n^{-1/2}z\big)^{1/2}\big(1+4n^{1/2}z+(z-n^{1/2})^{2}\big)^{-1/2}(1+(z-n^{1/2})^{2})^{-1}n^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(0)\int_{2\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}n^{-1/4}z^{1/2}\big(z-n^{1/2}\big)^{-1}\,\big(z-n^{1/2}\big)^{-2}\,n^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(0)\,n^{1/4}\int_{2\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}z^{1/2}\big(z-z/2\big)^{-3}\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad=c(0)\,n^{1/4}\int_{2\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}z^{-5/2}\,\mathrm{d}z=c(0)\,n^{-1/2}.

Combining
6
,
6.7
and the last three formulas yields

\int_{-2\sqrt{n}}^{2\sqrt{n}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(1+\|\gamma_{x,n}(z)\|\big)^{-m}\big(n^{-1/2}z\big)^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\leq c(0).

This remains valid if
x
is replaced by
x-h
for any
|h|\leq a_{n}
and thus

\displaystyle\sup_{|h|\leq a_{n}}\int_{-2\sqrt{n}}^{2\sqrt{n}}R_{n}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(1+\|\gamma_{x,n}(z)\|\big)^{-m}\big(n^{-1/2}z\big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\big(1+\|\gamma_{x-h,n}(z)\|\big)^{-m}\big(n^{-1/2}z\big)^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\quad\leq R_{n}^{\prime}\,c(0)=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-(m-2)/2}\big),

which yields
6.3
when combined with
6.4
and
6.5
.
∎

Theorem

4.1
implies
2
.
Due to the form
1.5
and the factor
\phi
in all expansion terms
q_{r}
,
\|\phi^{q}_{m,n}\|_{L^{p}}\leq c(m)
for all
p\in[1,\infty)
. Thus by
1.9
,
\|f_{n}\|_{L^{p}}\leq c(m)
for all
p\in[1,\infty)
and all
n
sufficiently large.
Set
{s}=m+1/2
and
a_{n}=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-(9m-16)/2}\big)
with polynomial order.
Now
Propositions

6.2
and
6.3
imply
6.1
.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

The bounds in the
L^{1}
limit theorem are by
\log n
tighter than in the LLT. This is because the integral modulus of continuity used in the
L^{1}
setting is easier to estimate than the pointwise Hölder-type bound which was the equivalent condition in the LLT setting. As this bound was used in the proof of the entropic CLT, the discrepancy of
\log n
transfers there. The reason behind the factor
\log n
is the appearance of different exponents in
Lemma

A.6
.

A
Proofs, remarks and auxiliary lemmas

A.1
Proof of Proposition
2.1

Remark A.1
.

We calculate the expected values of the
\widetilde{\lambda}_{l,n}
appearing in
\widetilde{P}_{2,n}
and
\widetilde{P}_{4,n}
(see
2.1
). For clear illustration, let all moments be finite in the following procedure. Additionally, we will explicitly write
\mu_{2}
instead of its fixed value 1 as it helps to keep track of the order of moments. First, we need the expected values of the conditional cumulants divided by powers of
V_{n}
for which we expand

\begin{split}V_{n}^{-2}&=n^{-1}\Big(\mu_{2}+n^{-1}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\Big)\Big)^{-1}\\
&=n^{-1}\mu_{2}^{-1}-n^{-2}\mu_{2}^{-2}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\Big)\\
&\quad+n^{-3}\mu_{2}^{-3}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\Big)^{2}+\mathcal{O}_{p}\big(n^{-3}\big)\end{split}

(A.1)

therefore

\displaystyle V_{n}^{-4}

\displaystyle=n^{-2}\mu_{2}^{-2}-n^{-3}2\mu_{2}^{-3}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\Big)+n^{-4}3\mu_{2}^{-4}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\Big)^{2}+\mathcal{O}_{p}\big(n^{-4}\big),

\displaystyle V_{n}^{-6}

\displaystyle=n^{-3}\mu_{2}^{-3}-n^{-4}3\mu_{2}^{-4}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\Big)+n^{-5}6\mu_{2}^{-5}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\Big)^{2}+\mathcal{O}_{p}\big(n^{-5}\big),

\displaystyle V_{n}^{-8}

\displaystyle=n^{-4}\mu_{2}^{-4}-n^{-5}4\mu_{2}^{-5}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\Big)+n^{-6}10\mu_{2}^{-6}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\Big)^{2}+\mathcal{O}_{p}\big(n^{-6}\big).

Due to
\mathbb{E}X_{j}^{2}=\mu_{2}
, the index of every factor
(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})
within

\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\Big)^{k}\sum|X_{j}|^{2l}=\sum_{j_{1},\dots,j_{k+1}=1}^{n}\big(X_{j_{1}}^{2}-\mu_{2}\big)\cdots\big(X_{j_{k}}^{2}-\mu_{2}\big)|X_{j_{k+1}}|^{2l}

has to be equal to the index of another factor or the summand vanishes within the expectation. Thus, for
k\in\mathbb{N}
, the expectation of summands with
\big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\big)^{2k-1}
and
{\big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\big)^{2k}}
produce the same order of
n
. This yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[V_{n}^{-4}\sum|X_{j}|^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\mu_{2}^{-2}\mu_{4}-n^{-2}\mu_{2}^{-4}\big(2\mu_{2}\mu_{6}+\mu_{2}^{2}\mu_{4}-3\mu_{4}^{2}\big)+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-3}\big),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[V_{n}^{-6}\sum|X_{j}|^{6}\Big]

\displaystyle=n^{-2}\mu_{2}^{-3}\mu_{6}-n^{-3}\mu_{2}^{-5}(3\mu_{8}\mu_{2}+3\mu_{6}\mu_{2}^{2}-6\mu_{4}\mu_{6})+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-4}\big),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[V_{n}^{-8}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{4}\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=n^{-2}\mu_{2}^{-4}\mu_{4}^{2}-n^{-3}\mu_{2}^{-5}(8\mu_{6}\mu_{4}-7\mu_{4}^{2}\mu_{2}-\mu_{8}\mu_{2})+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-4}\big)

such that by
2.4
and
2.5

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\widetilde{\lambda}_{4,n}}{4!}\bigg]

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\big(-\tfrac{1}{12}\big)\mu_{2}^{-2}\mu_{4}+n^{-2}\tfrac{1}{12}\mu_{2}^{-4}\big(2\mu_{2}\mu_{6}+\mu_{2}^{2}\mu_{4}-3\mu_{4}^{2}\big)+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-3}\big),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\widetilde{\lambda}_{6,n}}{6!}\bigg]

\displaystyle=n^{-2}\tfrac{1}{45}\mu_{2}^{-3}\mu_{6}+n^{-3}\big(-\tfrac{1}{45}\big)\mu_{2}^{-5}(3\mu_{8}\mu_{2}+3\mu_{6}\mu_{2}^{2}-6\mu_{4}\mu_{6})+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-4}\big),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{\widetilde{\lambda}_{4,n}}{4!}\Big)^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle=n^{-2}\tfrac{1}{288}\mu_{2}^{-4}\mu_{4}^{2}+n^{-3}\big(-\tfrac{1}{288}\big)\mu_{2}^{-5}(8\mu_{6}\mu_{4}-7\mu_{4}^{2}\mu_{2}-\mu_{8}\mu_{2})+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-4}\big).

The connection
\mathbb{E}\big[\Phi^{\widetilde{P}}_{m,n}\big]
and
\Phi^{Q}_{m,n}
(the procedure for
\mathbb{E}\big[\phi^{\widetilde{p}}_{m,n}\big]
and
\phi^{q}_{m,n}
is the same) is given in
2.8
,
2.9
,
2.12
and
1.1
and the corresponding computations are conducted in
Remark

A.1
. However, we need to address the issue of moments higher than
{s}
(as they could be infinite) and the factor
\exp(x^{2}/4)
. For
{s}<4
, there are no expansion terms in
2.15
and
2.16
and there is nothing to prove. So assume
{s}\geq 4
.

As in
Remark

A.1
, we first evaluate the expectation of
V_{n}^{-2\alpha}\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}
for
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}
,
{2\leq\alpha\leq m/2}
. Define
{h}=2\lceil\frac{m-2\alpha+1}{2}\rceil
. Similar to
A.1
, we perform a Taylor expansion of
V_{n}^{-2\alpha}
up to order
{h}-1
as follows

\begin{split}V_{n}^{-2\alpha}\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}&=\sum_{r=0}^{{h}-1}(-1)^{r}n^{-(\alpha+r)}t_{r,2\alpha}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-1)\Big)^{r}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\\
&\quad+(-1)^{{h}}n^{-(\alpha+{h})}t_{{h},2\alpha}\xi^{-(\alpha+{h})}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-1)\Big)^{{h}}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big),\end{split}

(A.2)

with (random) intermediate value
\xi
between
1
and
n^{-1}V_{n}^{2}
. Here,
t_{r,2\alpha}
are the constant factors which appear in this Taylor expansion and are bounded in absolute value by
c(m)
.

First, we will show that the sum of all terms where
X_{\cdot}
appears with a power higher than
{s}
is of order
\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)
. The same is shown for the remainder of the Taylor expansion including the
\xi
.

Note that by
2.2
,
3.31
and
3.32
, we can restrict the expectation of the terms in
A.2
to the event

A_{n,1}\cap A_{n,2}\quad\text{where}\quad A_{n,1}:=\{M_{n}<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\quad\text{and}\quad A_{n,2}:=\{V_{n}^{2}>\tfrac{n}{2}\}

(A.3)

with an error of order
\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)
. The sequence
(\delta_{n})
is introduced in
Remark

3.7
.
Therefore,

\begin{split}\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[V_{n}^{-2\alpha}\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big]$\mbox{}\hfil\quad\\
&=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}\cap A_{n,2}}V_{n}^{-2\alpha}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\Big]+\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)\\
&=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}\cap A_{n,2}}\sum_{r=0}^{{h}-1}(-1)^{r}n^{-(\alpha+r)}t_{r,2\alpha}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-1)\Big)^{r}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\bigg]\\
&\quad+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}\cap A_{n,2}}(-1)^{{h}}n^{-(\alpha+{h})}t_{{h},2\alpha}\xi^{-(\alpha+{h})}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-1)\Big)^{{h}}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\bigg]\\
&\quad+\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big).\end{split}

(A.4)

Note that by
A.3

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}\cap A_{n,2}^{c}}\sum_{r=0}^{{h}-1}(-1)^{r}n^{-(\alpha+r)}t_{r,2\alpha}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-1)\Big)^{r}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\bigg]\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq c(h)\sum_{r=0}^{{h}-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}\cap A_{n,2}^{c}}n^{-(\alpha+r)}\big(V_{n}^{2}+n\big)^{r}V_{n}^{2}(\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,)^{2\alpha-2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq c(h)\sum_{r=0}^{{h}-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,2}^{c}}n^{-(r+1)}\big(\tfrac{n}{2}+n\big)^{r}\tfrac{n}{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq c(h)\mathbb{P}\big(A_{n,2}^{c}\big)

\displaystyle=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)

and thus,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}\cap A_{n,2}}\sum_{r=0}^{{h}-1}(-1)^{r}n^{-(\alpha+r)}t_{r,2\alpha}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-1)\Big)^{r}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\bigg]\\
&=\sum_{r=0}^{{h}-1}(-1)^{r}n^{-(\alpha+r)}t_{r,2\alpha}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-1)\Big)^{r}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\Big]+\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big).\end{split}

(A.5)

Initially, we examine

n^{-(\alpha+r)}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-1)\Big)^{r}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\Big]

for
r=0,\dots,m
.

We expand

\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-1)\Big)^{r}=\sum_{i_{1}+\dots+i_{n}=r}\binom{r}{i_{1},\dots,i_{n}}\prod(X_{j}^{2}-1)^{i_{j}}.

Here the sum extends over all
n
-tuples
(i_{1},...,i_{n})
of non-negative integers with
\sum_{j=1}^{n}i_{j}=r
and

{r\choose i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}}={\frac{r!}{i_{1}!\,i_{2}!\cdots i_{n}!}}.

Since
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
are i.i.d.,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[{r\choose i_{\pi(1)},\ldots,i_{\pi(n)}}\prod(X_{j}^{2}-1)^{i_{\pi(j)}}\bigg]=\mathbb{E}\bigg[{r\choose i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}}\prod(X_{j}^{2}-1)^{i_{j}}\bigg]

for all (non-random) permutations
\pi
from
\{1,\dots,n\}
to itself.

For
l=1,\dots,r
let
k_{l}
be the number of appearances of
l
in the tuple
(i_{1},...,i_{n})
(so
\sum_{l=1}^{r}lk_{l}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}i_{j}=r
). Then for each tuple
(k_{1},\dots,k_{r})
, there exist
{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-\sum_{l=1}^{r}k_{l})}
tuples
(i_{1},...,i_{n})
such that
k_{l}
is the number of appearances of
l
in the tuple
(i_{1},...,i_{n})
. So instead of summing over all
n
-tuples
(i_{1},...,i_{n})
with
\sum_{j=1}^{n}i_{j}=r
, we can sum over all
r
-tuples
(k_{1},...,k_{r})
of non-negative integers with
\sum_{l=1}^{r}lk_{l}=r
and multiply each summand by
{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-\sum_{l=1}^{r}k_{l})}
. We perform the just described summation by using the sum
\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}
and
u(k_{\cdot})
which are explained in
Section

2
. Additionally, we convert

{r\choose i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}}={\frac{r!}{i_{1}!\,i_{2}!\cdots i_{n}!}}=\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}.

Now for any
(k_{1},\dots,k_{r})
, we identify that element of the corresponding set

\{(i_{\pi(1)},...,i_{\pi(n)}):\pi\text{ permutations from }\{1,\dots,n\}\text{ to itself}\}

for which

\displaystyle i_{1}

\displaystyle=\dots=i_{k_{1}}=1,

\displaystyle i_{k_{1}+1}

\displaystyle=\dots=i_{k_{1}+k_{2}}=2,

\displaystyle i_{\sum_{l=1}^{r-1}k_{l}+1}

\displaystyle=\dots=i_{\sum_{l=1}^{r}k_{l}}=r,

\displaystyle i_{\sum_{l=1}^{r}k_{l}+1}

\displaystyle=\dots=i_{n}=0.

When summing over
(k_{1},\dots,k_{r})
, this will be the representative, evaluated within the expectation. This reordering yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-1)\Big)^{r}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i_{1}+\dots+i_{n}=r}\binom{r}{i_{1},\dots,i_{n}}\Big(\prod(X_{j}^{2}-1)^{i_{j}}\Big)\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\cdot\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\prod_{a=1}^{k_{l}}\Big(X_{\sum_{b=1}^{l-1}k_{b}+a}^{2}-1\Big)^{l}\bigg)\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\Bigg].

As
M_{n}
is invariant under permutation, the last set of formulas remains valid if
\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}}
is present in the expectation. So

\begin{split}&n^{-(\alpha+r)}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-1)\Big)^{r}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\Big]\\
&\quad=n^{-(\alpha+r)}\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}\\
&\quad\qquad\cdot\sum\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\prod_{a=1}^{k_{l}}\Big(X_{\sum_{b=1}^{l-1}k_{b}+a}^{2}-1\Big)^{l}\bigg)|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\bigg]\end{split}

(A.6)

for all
r=0,\dots,m
.

Below, we distinguish between the values of
j
, which only coincide with the index of another factor for
j\leq u(k_{\cdot})
, and get

\begin{split}&\sum\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\prod_{a=1}^{k_{l}}\Big(X_{\sum_{b=1}^{l-1}k_{b}+a}^{2}-1\Big)^{l}\bigg)|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\bigg]\\
&\quad=\sum_{j=u(k_{\cdot})+1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\prod_{a=1}^{k_{l}}\Big(X_{\sum_{b=1}^{l-1}k_{b}+a}^{2}-1\Big)^{l}\bigg)|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\bigg]\\
&\quad\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{u(k_{\cdot})}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\prod_{a=1}^{k_{l}}\Big(X_{\sum_{b=1}^{l-1}k_{b}+a}^{2}-1\Big)^{l}\bigg)|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\bigg].\end{split}

(A.7)

Next, we use that
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
are i.i.d. and

A_{n,1}=\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\cap\dots\cap\{|X_{n}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}.

(A.8)

Additionally, we introduce the notation

\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}[\,\cdot\,]:=\mathbb{E}[\mathbbm{1}_{\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}}\,\cdot\,].

For the upper part of the sum this yields

\displaystyle\sum_{j=u(k_{\cdot})+1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\prod_{a=1}^{k_{l}}\Big(X_{\sum_{b=1}^{l-1}k_{b}+a}^{2}-1\Big)^{l}\bigg)|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\big(n-u(k_{\cdot})\big)\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})-1}.

Assume that
k_{l}>0
for at least one
l\geq\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1
(i.e.
2l>{s}
). Furthermore, in the following formula we use the fact
2\leq\alpha\leq m/2
. As

{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\leq c(r)n^{u(k_{\cdot})}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\big(n-u(k_{\cdot})\big)\leq n,

these summands of
\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}
in
A.6
can be bounded by

\displaystyle c(r)n^{-\alpha-r+u(k_{\cdot})+1}\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})-1}

\displaystyle=c(r)n^{1-\alpha}\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\Big(n^{1-l}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})-1}

\displaystyle=c(r)n^{1-\alpha}\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{\lfloor s/2\rfloor}\Big(n^{1-l}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\bigg(\prod_{l=\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}^{r}\Big(n^{1-l}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})-1}

\displaystyle\leq c(r)n^{1-\alpha}\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{\lfloor s/2\rfloor}\Big(n^{1-l}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\bigg(\prod_{l=\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}^{r}\Big(n^{1-\lfloor s/2\rfloor}\delta_{n}^{2(l-\lfloor s/2\rfloor)}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{\lfloor s/2\rfloor}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})-1}

\displaystyle\leq c(r)n^{1-\alpha+1-\lfloor s/2\rfloor}\delta_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big).

Thus, the upper part of the sum reduces to

\begin{split}&n^{-(\alpha+r)}\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}\big(n-u(k_{\cdot})\big)\mathbbm{1}_{\{k_{\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}=\dots=k_{r}=0\}}\\
&\qquad\cdot\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})-1}\\
&\quad+\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big).\end{split}

(A.9)

Now to the lower part of the sum in
A.7
. We treat the summands separately. For any
j
, define
l^{*}=l^{*}(j)\in\{1,\dots,r\}
as the power of the term
(X_{j}^{2}-1)
within the products in
A.7
. in turn, for any
l^{*}
, let
j^{*}
be the smallest index with the power
l^{*}
, i.e.
j^{*}=j^{*}(l^{*}):=\argmin\{j:l^{*}(j)=l^{*}\}
. With this assignment
j^{*}=\sum_{b=1}^{{l^{*}}-1}k_{b}+1
.

We use that
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
are i.i.d. and
, which yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\prod_{a=1}^{k_{l}}\Big(X_{\sum_{b=1}^{l-1}k_{b}+a}^{2}-1\Big)^{l}\bigg)|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\prod_{a=1}^{k_{l}}\Big(X_{\sum_{b=1}^{l-1}k_{b}+a}^{2}-1\Big)^{l}\bigg)|X_{\sum_{b=1}^{{l^{*}}-1}k_{b}+1}|^{2\alpha}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l^{*}}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l^{*}}-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})}.

Let
2\alpha+2l^{*}>{s}
. These summands of
\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}
in
A.6
can be bounded by

\displaystyle c(r)n^{-\alpha-r+u(k_{\cdot})}\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l^{*}}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l^{*}}-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})}

\displaystyle=c(r)n^{-\alpha}\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l^{*}}^{r}\Big(n^{1-l}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\Big(n^{1-l^{*}}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l^{*}}-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot n^{1-l^{*}}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})}

\displaystyle\leq c(r)n^{-\alpha}\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l^{*}}^{r}\Big(\delta_{n}^{2(l-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\Big(\delta_{n}^{2(l^{*}-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)\Big]\Big)^{k_{l^{*}}-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot n^{1-l^{*}}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(|X_{1}|^{2l^{*}+2\alpha}+|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\big)\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})}

\displaystyle\leq c(r)n^{-\alpha}\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l^{*}}^{r}\Big(\delta_{n}^{2(l-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\Big(\delta_{n}^{2(l^{*}-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)\Big]\Big)^{k_{l^{*}}-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot n^{1-l^{*}}(\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,)^{2l^{*}+2\alpha-{s}}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(|X_{1}|^{s}+|X_{1}|^{{s}-2l^{*}}\big)\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})}

\displaystyle\leq c(r)n^{-\alpha+1-l^{*}+l^{*}+\alpha-{s}/2}\delta_{n}^{2l^{*}+2\alpha-{s}}

\displaystyle=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)

since
\delta_{n}\to 0
.

If
2\alpha+2l^{*}\leq{s}
, a similar procedure and bound as for the upper part of the sum in
A.7
apply. To this end, assume that
k_{l}>0
for at least one
l\geq\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1
. Furthermore, in the following formula we use the fact
2\leq\alpha\leq m/2
. With
l^{*}
as above, these summands of
A.6
can be bounded by

\displaystyle c(r)n^{-\alpha-r+u(k_{\cdot})}\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l^{*}}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l^{*}}-1}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})}

\displaystyle=c(r)n^{-\alpha}\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l^{*}}^{r}\Big(n^{1-l}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\Big(n^{1-l^{*}}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l^{*}}-1}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot n^{1-l^{*}}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})}

\displaystyle=c(r)n^{-\alpha}\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l^{*}}^{\lfloor s/2\rfloor}\Big(n^{1-l}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\Big(n^{1-l^{*}}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l^{*}}-1}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot n^{1-l^{*}}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\bigg(\prod_{l=\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}^{r}\Big(n^{1-l}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})}

\displaystyle\leq c(r)n^{-\alpha}\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l^{*}}^{\lfloor s/2\rfloor}\Big(n^{1-l}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\Big(n^{1-l^{*}}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l^{*}}-1}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot n^{1-l^{*}}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\bigg(\prod_{l=\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}^{r}\Big(n^{1-\lfloor s/2\rfloor}\delta_{n}^{2(l-\lfloor s/2\rfloor)}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{\lfloor s/2\rfloor}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})}

\displaystyle\leq c(r)n^{-\alpha+1-l^{*}+1-\lfloor s/2\rfloor}\delta_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big).

Summarizing, for the summands of
\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}
in
A.6
with
j\leq u(k_{\cdot})
we get

\displaystyle n^{-(\alpha+r)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\prod_{a=1}^{k_{l}}\Big(X_{\sum_{b=1}^{l-1}k_{b}+a}^{2}-1\Big)^{l}\bigg)|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad=n^{-(\alpha+r)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{k_{\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}=\dots=k_{r}=0,\,2\alpha+2l^{*}\leq{s}\}}

(A.10)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\cdot\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l^{*}}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l^{*}}-1}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big).

Putting together
A.9
,
A.1
,
A.7
and
A.6
, we get

\displaystyle n^{-(\alpha+r)}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-1)\Big)^{r}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\Big]

(A.11)

\displaystyle=n^{-(\alpha+r)}\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}\big(n-u(k_{\cdot})\big)\mathbbm{1}_{\{k_{\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}=\dots=k_{r}=0\}}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})-1}

\displaystyle\quad+n^{-(\alpha+r)}\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{u(k_{\cdot})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{k_{\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}=\dots=k_{r}=0,\,2\alpha+2l^{*}\leq{s}\}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l^{*}}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l^{*}}-1}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big).

Note that for
a=0,\dots,\lfloor s/2\rfloor
and
b=0,\dots,\lfloor s/2\rfloor
with
2a+2b\leq{s}
, the expectations including the complementary indicator are bounded by

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\{|X_{1}|\geq\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}}\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{a}|X_{1}|^{2b}\Big]\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\{|X_{1}|\geq\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}}\big(|X_{1}|^{2a}+1\big)|X_{1}|^{2b}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\{|X_{1}|\geq\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}}(\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,)^{-(s-2a-2b)}\big(|X_{1}|^{s}+|X_{1}|^{s-2a}\big)\Big]

(A.12)

\displaystyle\leq c(0)n^{-(s/2-a-b)}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\{|X_{1}|\geq\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}}\delta_{n}^{-s}|X_{1}|^{s}\Big]

\displaystyle=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-(s/2-a-b)}\big)

by
3.30
. In particular for
a=b=0
,
\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|\geq\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-s/2}\big)
.

For any
l_{0}=0,\dots,\lfloor s/2\rfloor
, the presence of a factor
\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l_{0}}\big]
in a summand of
A.11
implies
k_{l_{0}}\geq 1
. Transitioning from
\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l_{0}}\big]
to
\mathbb{E}\big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l_{0}}\big]
in one of its appearances leads us to study the additional summand in
A.11

\displaystyle n^{-(\alpha+r)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}\big(n-u(k_{\cdot})\big)\mathbbm{1}_{\{k_{\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}=\dots=k_{r}=0\}}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l_{0}}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l_{0}}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l_{0}}-1}\bigg(-\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\{|X_{1}|\geq\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}}\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l_{0}}\Big]\bigg)

in the upper part and in the lower part correspondingly.
Using
A.1
, this summand is bounded in absolute value by

\displaystyle c(m)n^{-(\alpha+r)}n^{u(k_{\cdot})}n\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\{|X_{1}|\geq\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}}\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l_{0}}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(m)n^{-\alpha-r+u(k_{\cdot})+1}\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-(s/2-l_{0})}\big)

\displaystyle\quad=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-\alpha-r+u(k_{\cdot})+1-s/2+l_{0}}\big).

Using the facts
\alpha\geq 2
and
k_{l_{0}}\geq 1
, we examine the power of
n

\displaystyle-\alpha-r+u(k_{\cdot})+1-s/2+l_{0}

\displaystyle\leq-2-r+u(k_{\cdot})+1-s/2+(l_{0}-1)k_{l_{0}}+1

\displaystyle=-s/2-r+(k_{1}+\dots+k_{l_{0}}+\dots+k_{r})+(l_{0}-1)k_{l_{0}}

\displaystyle=-s/2-r+(k_{1}+\dots+l_{0}k_{l_{0}}+\dots+k_{r})

\displaystyle\leq-s/2.

Thus, leaving out the indicator in the factor
\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l_{0}}\big]
produces an error of order
\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-s/2}\big)
in the upper part of
A.11
. In the lower part, the factor
n-u(k_{\cdot})
is missing and the order therefore even smaller.

Repeating this argument consecutively (note that this requires
c(r)
steps at most) until no
\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\big]
is left anymore, we arrive at

\displaystyle n^{-(\alpha+r)}\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}\big(n-u(k_{\cdot})\big)\mathbbm{1}_{\{k_{\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}=\dots=k_{r}=0\}}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\cdot\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})-1}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+n^{-(\alpha+r)}\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\cdot\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{u(k_{\cdot})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{k_{\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}=\dots=k_{r}=0,\,2\alpha+2l^{*}\leq{s}\}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l^{*}}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l^{*}}-1}\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)^{n-u(k_{\cdot})}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big).

Similar in spirit, we consecutively transition from
\mathbb{P}\big(\{|X_{1}|\geq\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}\big)
to 1 at the cost of at most
\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-{s}/2}\big)
, from
\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]
to
\mathbb{E}\Big[|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]
at the cost of at most
\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)
because

\displaystyle-\alpha-r+u(k_{\cdot})+1-s/2+\alpha\leq-(s-2)/2,

and from
\mathbb{E}_{\delta_{n},n}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]
to
\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]
at the cost of at most
\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)
because

\displaystyle-\alpha-r+u(k_{\cdot})-s/2+{l^{*}}+\alpha

\displaystyle\leq-s/2-r+(k_{1}+\dots+k_{l^{*}}+\dots+k_{r})+({l^{*}}-1)k_{l^{*}}+1

\displaystyle=-s/2-r+(k_{1}+\dots+{l^{*}}k_{l^{*}}+\dots+k_{r})+1

\displaystyle\leq-(s-2)/2.

As a consequence, we get from
A.11

\displaystyle n^{-(\alpha+r)}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-1)\Big)^{r}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=n^{-(\alpha+r)}\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\big(n-u(k_{\cdot})\big)\mathbbm{1}_{\{k_{\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}=\dots=k_{r}=0\}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\mathbb{E}\Big[|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+n^{-(\alpha+r)}\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}

(A.13)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{u(k_{\cdot})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{k_{\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}=\dots=k_{r}=0,\,2\alpha+2l^{*}\leq{s}\}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l^{*}}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l^{*}}-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big).

Now we return to the remainder of the Taylor expansion in
A.4
and shall prove that this term is of order
\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)
. Recall that
\xi
is a (random) intermediate value between
1
and
n^{-1}V_{n}^{2}
and
{h}=2\lceil\frac{m-2\alpha+1}{2}\rceil
. Note that
\xi\geq 1/2
on
A_{n,2}
and since
2\leq\alpha\leq m/2
,
\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,2}}\xi^{-(\alpha+{h})}\leq c(m)
. Together with
|t_{{h},2\alpha}|\leq c(m)
, this yields

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}\cap A_{n,2}}(-1)^{{h}}n^{-(\alpha+{h})}t_{{h},2\alpha}\xi^{-(\alpha+{h})}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-1)\Big)^{{h}}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\Big]\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(m)n^{-(\alpha+{h})}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n,1}}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-1)\Big)^{{h}}\Big(\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big)\Big].

If we trace through the arguments above, we see that they are valid for all
r=0,\dots,m
. Thus, all steps are also valid for the remainder of the Taylor expansion, where
r={h}
. By
A.1
, the expression above thus is equal to

\displaystyle c(m)\Bigg(n^{-(\alpha+r)}\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\big(n-u(k_{\cdot})\big)\mathbbm{1}_{\{k_{\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}=\dots=k_{r}=0\}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\mathbb{E}\Big[|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+n^{-(\alpha+r)}\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}

(A.14)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{u(k_{\cdot})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{k_{\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}=\dots=k_{r}=0,\,2\alpha+2l^{*}\leq{s}\}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l^{*}}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l^{*}}-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)

with
r={h}
.
Note that
\mathbb{E}X_{1}^{2}=1
. Thus in the first part, all summands with
k_{1}>0
vanish and in the second part, all summands with
k_{1}>1
vanish. This results in

u(k_{\cdot})=k_{1}+k_{2}+\dots+k_{r}=\tfrac{1}{2}(2k_{2}+\dots+2k_{r})\leq r/2

for the first part and

u(k_{\cdot})=k_{1}+k_{2}+\dots+k_{r}\leq 1+\tfrac{1}{2}(2k_{2}+\dots+2k_{r})\leq 1+r/2

for the second part.

Now we want to determine the order in
n
of the terms in
A.1
. Note that all appearing moments are finite. Using
r={h}\geq m-2\alpha+1
, we can focus on the power of
n
which is in the first part

\displaystyle-\alpha-r+u(k_{\cdot})+1

\displaystyle\leq-\alpha-r+r/2+1=-\alpha-r/2+1

\displaystyle\leq-\alpha-(m-2\alpha+1)/2+1=-(m-1)/2

and in the second part

\displaystyle-\alpha-r+u(k_{\cdot})\leq-\alpha-r+1+r/2=-\alpha-r/2+1\leq-(m-1)/2.

Thus, all terms (at most
c(m)
many) from
A.1
are of order
\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)
.
Ultimately, merging
A.4
,
,
A.1
and
A.1
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[V_{n}^{-2\alpha}\sum|X_{j}|^{2\alpha}\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{{h}-1}(-1)^{r}n^{-(\alpha+r)}t_{r,2\alpha}\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\big(n-u(k_{\cdot})\big)\mathbbm{1}_{\{k_{\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}=\dots=k_{r}=0\}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)\mathbb{E}\Big[|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{r=0}^{{h}-1}(-1)^{r}n^{-(\alpha+r)}t_{r,2\alpha}\sum_{*(k_{\cdot},r,\cdot)}{n\choose k_{1},\ldots,k_{r},(n-u(k_{\cdot}))}\frac{r!}{1!^{k_{1}}\cdots r!^{k_{r}}}

(A.15)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{u(k_{\cdot})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{k_{\lfloor s/2\rfloor+1}=\dots=k_{r}=0,\,2\alpha+2l^{*}\leq{s}\}}\bigg(\prod_{l=1,~l\neq l^{*}}^{r}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}\Big]\Big)^{k_{l^{*}}-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{1}^{2}-1\big)^{l^{*}}|X_{1}|^{2\alpha}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big).

All expectations in
A.1
can be calculated explicitly as no power of
X_{1}
is greater than
s
. Using
u(k_{\cdot})\leq r,\alpha\in\mathbb{N}
and
\alpha\geq 2
, this results in an expansion in terms of orders
n^{-1},n^{-2},\dots,n^{-\lfloor m-2/2\rfloor}
with moments up to order
m
and a remainder of order
\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)
. By
2.4
and
2.5
for
k\geq 4
, the expectation of
\widetilde{\lambda}_{k,n}
and its powers admit the same expansion up to the respective coefficient from
2.4
. In
Remark

A.1
, this expansion is performed exemplarily for
k=4
and
k=6
. By
2.8
and
2.9
,
\mathbb{E}\big[\Phi^{\widetilde{P}}_{m,n}(x)\big]
admits a similar representation. By the definition of
Q_{r}
in
2.12
, the expansion terms of order
n^{-r/2}
are assigned to their respective
Q_{r}
(or to the remainder
\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)
). Note that
Q_{r}=0
for uneven
r
. That is, all the non-remainder terms in

\mathbb{E}\big[\Phi^{\widetilde{P}}_{m,n}(x)\big]

are exactly the summands appearing in
\Phi^{Q}_{m,n}
, defined in
1.1
, and therefore cancel each other out in
2.15
and
2.16
.
What still needs to be examined is the dependence of the remainder on the argument
x
. In
\widetilde{P}_{k,n}
(as well as in
\widetilde{p}_{k,n}
), the
\widetilde{\lambda}_{k,n}
are multiplied by
\phi
and a Hermite polynomial of order
l\leq 2m
. For
l\leq 2m
, we can bound

\sup_{x}\exp(x^{2}/4)\phi(x)H_{l}(x)\leq c(l).

As this non-random factor remains unaffected by the expectation, it also appears in the remainder such that
2.15
and
2.16
hold.
∎

A.2
Further proofs for Section
3

We prove this lemma by an approach similar to the one used in the classical proof of Markov’s inequality. Without loss of generality let
a_{1}=\max\{a_{1},\dots,a_{l}\}
. By using
M_{n,l}=\max_{j=l+1,l+2,\dots,n}|X_{j}|
and
j_{0}\in\{1,\dots,n\}
being the smallest index with
|X_{j_{0}}|=M_{n}
, we see

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\big\{M_{n}\geq\sqrt{\frac{n}{\log(n)\,\eta}}\big\}}|X_{1}|^{a_{1}}\cdots|X_{l}|^{a_{l}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\{j_{0}=j\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\big\{M_{n}\geq\sqrt{\frac{n}{\log(n)\,\eta}}\big\}}|X_{1}|^{a_{1}}\cdots|X_{l}|^{a_{l}}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=l+1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{j_{0}=j\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\big\{M_{n}\geq\sqrt{\frac{n}{\log(n)\,\eta}}\big\}}|X_{1}|^{a_{1}}\cdots|X_{l}|^{a_{l}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq l\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\{j_{0}=1\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\big\{M_{n}\geq\sqrt{\frac{n}{\log(n)\,\eta}}\big\}}|X_{1}|^{a_{1}}\cdots|X_{l}|^{a_{l}}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\{j_{0}>l\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\big\{M_{n}\geq\sqrt{\frac{n}{\log(n)\,\eta}}\big\}}|X_{1}|^{a_{1}}\cdots|X_{l}|^{a_{l}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\,l\,\big(\tfrac{n}{\log(n)\,\eta}\big)^{-({s}-a_{1})/2}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\{j_{0}=1\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\big\{M_{n}\geq\sqrt{\frac{n}{\log(n)\,\eta}}\big\}}|X_{1}|^{{s}}|X_{2}|^{a_{2}}\cdots|X_{l}|^{a_{l}}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\{j_{0}>l\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\big\{M_{n,l}\geq\sqrt{\frac{n}{\log(n)\,\eta}}\big\}}|X_{1}|^{a_{1}}\cdots|X_{l}|^{a_{l}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\,l\,\big(\tfrac{n}{\log(n)\,\eta}\big)^{-({s}-a_{1})/2}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[|X_{1}|^{{s}}|X_{2}|^{a_{2}}\cdots|X_{l}|^{a_{l}}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\big\{M_{n,l}\geq\sqrt{\frac{n}{\log(n)\,\eta}}\big\}}|X_{1}|^{a_{1}}\cdots|X_{l}|^{a_{l}}\Big]

\displaystyle=l\,\eta^{({s}-a_{1})/2}\,(\log n)^{({s}-a_{1})/2}n^{-({s}-a_{1})/2}\mathbb{E}|X_{1}|^{{s}}\mathbb{E}|X_{1}|^{a_{2}}\cdots\mathbb{E}|X_{1}|^{a_{l}}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{P}\big(M_{n,l}\geq\sqrt{\tfrac{n}{\log(n)\,\eta}}\,\big)\mathbb{E}|X_{1}|^{a_{1}}\cdots\mathbb{E}|X_{1}|^{a_{l}}

\displaystyle=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\Big(n^{-({s}-\max\{2,a_{1}\})/2}\,(\log n)^{{s}/2}\Big),

where we used
3.29
in the last step.
∎

Using
(\delta_{n})
from
Remark

3.7
, we get by
2.2
,
3.32
and
3.31

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{L}_{k,n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{V_{n}^{2}\leq\frac{n}{2}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{M_{n}\geq\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{V_{n}^{2}>n/2,M_{n}<\delta_{n}\sqrt{n}\,\}}V_{n}^{-k}M_{n}^{k-{s}}\sum|X_{j}|^{{s}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+2^{k/2}n^{-({s}-2)/2}\delta_{n}^{k-{s}}\mathbb{E}\big[|X_{1}|^{{s}}\big]

\displaystyle=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)

since
\delta_{n}\to 0
.
∎

We split up the expectation and get by
3.32
and
3.29

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\big(-\eta\,B_{n}^{2}\big)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{V_{n}^{2}\leq\frac{n}{2}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\big\{M_{n}\geq\sqrt{\frac{n\,\eta}{\log(n)\,{s}}}\big\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\big\{V_{n}^{2}>n/2,M_{n}<\sqrt{\frac{n\,\eta}{\log(n)\,{s}}}\big\}}\exp\big(-\eta\,V_{n}^{2}\,M_{n}^{-2}\big)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(V_{n}^{2}\leq\tfrac{n}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{P}\left(M_{n}\geq\sqrt{\tfrac{n\,\eta}{\log(n)\,{s}}}\,\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\big\{V_{n}^{2}>n/2,M_{n}<\sqrt{\frac{n\,\eta}{\log(n)\,{s}}}\big\}}\exp\big(-\eta\,\tfrac{n}{2}\,\tfrac{\log(n)\,s}{n\,\eta}\big)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\,(\log n)^{{s}/2}(\eta\wedge 1)^{-s/2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+n^{-{s}/2}

\displaystyle=\begin{smallmatrix}\!{\mathcal{O}}\!\end{smallmatrix}\big(n^{-({s}-2)/2}\,(\log n)^{{s}/2}(\eta\wedge 1)^{-s/2}\big).

∎

A.3
Further proofs for Section
4

Take
f
to be the density of
X_{1}
,
Y_{j}:=(X_{j},X_{j}^{2})
,
B:=B_{1}\times B_{2}:=[a_{1},b_{1}]\times[a_{2},b_{2}]
with
a_{1}<b_{1}
,
a_{2}<b_{2}
in
\mathbb{R}
. Then by substitution and Fubini’s theorem,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(Y_{1}+Y_{2}+Y_{3}\in B\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big(X_{1}+X_{2}+X_{3}\in B_{1},X_{1}^{2}+X_{2}^{2}+X_{3}^{2}\in B_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(x)f(y)f(z)\mathbbm{1}\big\{x+y+z\in B_{1},x^{2}+y^{2}+z^{2}\in B_{2},x\leq y\big\}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(s-y-z)f(y)f(z)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\cdot\mathbbm{1}\big\{s\in B_{1},(s-y-z)^{2}+y^{2}+z^{2}\in B_{2},(s-z)/2\leq y\big\}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=2\int_{B_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(s-y-z)f(y)f(z)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\cdot\mathbbm{1}\big\{(s-y-z)^{2}+y^{2}+z^{2}\in B_{2},(s-z)/2\leq y\big\}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}s.

Define

v_{s,z}(y):=(s-y-z)^{2}+y^{2}+z^{2}=\tfrac{1}{2}\Big(\big(2y-(s-z)\big)^{2}+s^{2}+3z^{2}-2sz\Big).

Note that

v^{\prime}_{s,z}(y)=2(2y-s+z).

As we only consider the regime
(s-z)/2\leq y
, the inverse is

v_{s,z}^{-1}(v)=\tfrac{1}{2}\Big(s-z+\sqrt{2v-s^{2}-3z^{2}+2sz}\Big)=\tfrac{1}{2}\Big(s-z+\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\sqrt{6v-2s^{2}-(3z-s)^{2}}\Big).

Thus the expression above is equal to

\displaystyle 2\int_{B_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{(s-z)/2}^{\infty}\frac{2(2v_{s,z}^{-1}(v_{s,z}(y))-s+z)}{2(2v_{s,z}^{-1}(v_{s,z}(y))-s+z)}f(s-v_{s,z}^{-1}(v_{s,z}(y))-z)f(v_{s,z}^{-1}(v_{s,z}(y)))f(z)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\cdot\mathbbm{1}\big\{v_{s,z}(y)\in B_{2}\big\}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=2\int_{B_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{v_{s,z}((s-z)/2)}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2(2v_{s,z}^{-1}(v)-s+z)}f\big(s-v_{s,z}^{-1}(v)-z\big)f(v_{s,z}^{-1}(v))f(z)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathbbm{1}\big\{v\in B_{2}\big\}\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{1}}\int_{B_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{2v_{s,z}^{-1}(v)-s+z}f\big(s-v_{s,z}^{-1}(v)-z\big)f(v_{s,z}^{-1}(v))f(z)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathbbm{1}\big\{v_{s,z}\big((s-z)/2\big)\leq v\big\}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s.

Hence the random variable
(Y_{1}+Y_{2}+Y_{3})
has the density

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{2v_{s,z}^{-1}(v)-s+z}f\big(s-v_{s,z}^{-1}(v)-z\big)f(v_{s,z}^{-1}(v))f(z)\mathbbm{1}\big\{v_{s,z}\big((s-z)/2\big)\leq v\big\}\,\mathrm{d}z

for
s,v\in\mathbb{R}
.
Note that

v_{s,z}((s-z)/2)\leq v\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad 6v-2s^{2}-(3z-s)^{2}\geq 0\quad\Rightarrow\quad 6v-2s^{2}\geq 0.

We examine the integral

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{2v_{s,z}^{-1}(v)-s+z}\mathbbm{1}\big\{v_{s,z}((s-z)/2)\leq v\big\}\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{6v-2s^{2}-(3z-s)^{2}}}\mathbbm{1}\big\{\tfrac{1}{2}\big(s^{2}+3z^{2}-2sz\big)\leq v\big\}\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{6v-2s^{2}-(3z-s)^{2}}}\mathbbm{1}\big\{\tfrac{1}{3}\big(s-\sqrt{6v-2s^{2}}\big)\leq z\leq\tfrac{1}{3}\big(s+\sqrt{6v-2s^{2}}\big)\big\}\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{\frac{1}{3}(s-\sqrt{6v-2s^{2}})}^{\frac{1}{3}(s+\sqrt{6v-2s^{2}})}\frac{\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{6v-2s^{2}-(3z-s)^{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\int_{-\sqrt{6v-2s^{2}}}^{\sqrt{6v-2s^{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{6v-2s^{2}-w^{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}w=\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{3}}

by
Lemma

A.9
.
Therefore for all bounded
f
,
(\sum X_{j},\sum X_{j}^{2})
has a bounded density for
n=3
(or equivalently, for all
n\geq 3
). Thus by
[
8
, Theorem 19.1]
,
1
is fulfilled.
∎

By the triangle inequality and
Proposition

3.2
, we get for
{k=0,\dots,m}

\displaystyle\Big|{\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k}}{\mathrm{d}t^{k}}}\Big(\widetilde{\varphi}_{T_{n}^{\prime}}(t)-\widetilde{\varphi}_{\Phi^{\widetilde{P}}_{m,n}}(t)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|{\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k}}{\mathrm{d}t^{k}}}\Big(\widetilde{\varphi}_{T_{n}^{\prime}}(t)-\widetilde{\varphi}_{T_{n}}(t)\Big)\Big|+c(m)\widetilde{L}_{m+1,n}e^{-t^{2}/6}\big(|t|^{m+1-k}+|t|^{3m-1+k}\big),

where we expand the first summand by the Leibniz rule as

\displaystyle\Big|{\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k}}{\mathrm{d}t^{k}}}\Big(\widetilde{\varphi}_{T_{n}^{\prime}}(t)-\widetilde{\varphi}_{T_{n}}(t)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\binom{k}{l}\Big|{\frac{\mathrm{d}^{l}}{\mathrm{d}t^{l}}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{T_{n}}(t)\Big|\Big|{\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k-l}}{\mathrm{d}t^{k-l}}}\exp(-\tfrac{1}{2}\beta_{n}t^{2})\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|{\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k}}{\mathrm{d}t^{k}}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{T_{n}}(t)\Big|\Big(1-\exp(-\tfrac{1}{2}\beta_{n}t^{2})\Big).

By
Proposition

3.2
,
2.17
and
2.2
,

\displaystyle\Big|{\frac{\mathrm{d}^{l}}{\mathrm{d}t^{l}}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{T_{n}}(t)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|{\frac{\mathrm{d}^{l}}{\mathrm{d}t^{l}}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\Phi^{\widetilde{P}}_{m,n}}(t)\Big|+c(m)\widetilde{L}_{m+1,n}e^{-t^{2}/6}\big(|t|^{m+1-l}+|t|^{3m-1+l}\big)

\displaystyle\leq c(m)e^{-t^{2}/2}\big(1+|t|^{2m-4+l}\big)+c(m)e^{-t^{2}/6}\big(|t|^{m+1-l}+|t|^{3m-1+l}\big)

\displaystyle\leq c(m)e^{-t^{2}/6}\big(1+|t|^{3m-1+l}\big)

for
l=0,\dots,m
. Additionally for
k>l
,

\Big|{\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k-l}}{\mathrm{d}t^{k-l}}}\exp(-\tfrac{1}{2}\beta_{n}t^{2})\Big|\leq c(k-l)\beta_{n}(1+|t|^{k-l})\exp(-\tfrac{1}{2}\beta_{n}t^{2})

and

\Big(1-\exp(-\tfrac{1}{2}\beta_{n}t^{2})\Big)\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\beta_{n}t^{2}.

Putting everything together yields

\displaystyle\Big|{\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k}}{\mathrm{d}t^{k}}}\Big(\widetilde{\varphi}_{T_{n}^{\prime}}(t)-\widetilde{\varphi}_{T_{n}}(t)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\binom{k}{l}\Big|{\frac{\mathrm{d}^{l}}{\mathrm{d}t^{l}}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{T_{n}}(t)\Big|\Big|{\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k-l}}{\mathrm{d}t^{k-l}}}\exp(-\tfrac{1}{2}\beta_{n}t^{2})\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|{\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k}}{\mathrm{d}t^{k}}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{T_{n}}(t)\Big|\Big(1-\exp(-\tfrac{1}{2}\beta_{n}t^{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\binom{k}{l}\Big(c(m)e^{-t^{2}/6}\big(1+|t|^{3m-1+l}\big)\Big)\Big(c(k-l)\beta_{n}(1+|t|^{k-l})\exp(-\tfrac{1}{2}\beta_{n}t^{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(c(m)e^{-t^{2}/6}\big(1+|t|^{3m-1+k}\big)\Big)\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\beta_{n}t^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq c(m)\beta_{n}e^{-t^{2}/6}\big(1+|t|^{3m+1+k}\big),

which proves
4.4
.
∎

A.4
Further remarks

Remark A.2
.

Let
\Sigma
be the covariance matrix of
(X_{1},X_{1}^{2})
and assume
\mu_{4}<\infty
. Then the following equivalences hold:

(X_{1},X_{1}^{2})
is non-degenerate

\Leftrightarrow

\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\big(a_{1}X_{1}+a_{2}X_{1}^{2})>0
for all
a=(a_{1},a_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
with
a\neq 0

\Leftrightarrow

a^{T}\Sigma a>0
for all
a\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
with
a\neq 0

\Leftrightarrow

\Sigma
is positive definite.

Remark A.3
.

In this remark, we do neither assume that
X_{1}
is symmetric, nor that
\mu_{2}=1
. Under these conditions, we investigate if
T_{n}
is normalized, expanding
Remark

5.2
. For clear illustration however, let all moments be finite in the following procedure. By
A.1
we expand

\begin{split}T_{n}^{2}&=n^{-1}\mu_{2}^{-1}S_{n}^{2}-n^{-2}\mu_{2}^{-2}S_{n}^{2}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\Big)\\
&\quad+n^{-3}\mu_{2}^{-3}S_{n}^{2}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\Big)^{2}+\mathcal{O}_{p}\big(n^{-2}\big).\end{split}

(A.16)

Due to
\mu_{1}=0
and
\mathbb{E}X_{j}^{2}=\mu_{2}
, the index of every factor within

\Big(\sum X_{j}\Big)^{2}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\Big)^{k}=\sum_{j_{1},\dots,j_{k+2}=1}^{n}X_{j_{1}}X_{j_{2}}\big(X_{j_{3}}^{2}-\mu_{2}\big)\cdots\big(X_{j_{k+2}}^{2}-\mu_{2}\big)

has to be equal to the index of another factor or the summand vanishes within the expectation. We take the expectation of each summand of
A.16
separately

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}S_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle=n\mu_{2},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[S_{n}^{2}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=n(\mu_{4}-\mu_{2}^{2}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[S_{n}^{2}\Big(\sum(X_{j}^{2}-\mu_{2})\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=n^{2}\big(\mu_{2}\mu_{4}-\mu_{2}^{3}+2\mu_{3}^{2}\big)+\mathcal{O}(n)

which leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}T_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle=1+n^{-1}2\mu_{2}^{-3}\mu_{3}^{2}+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-2}\big).

A.5
Auxiliary results

Lemma A.4
.

Let
\nu_{n}\geq 1
,
k=0,1,\dots
and
0<\beta<4
. Then

\int_{\nu_{n}}^{\infty}t^{k}\exp\big(-\beta t^{2}/2\big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c(k)\beta^{-(k+2)/2}\exp\big(-\beta\nu_{n}^{2}/4\big).

We examine

\displaystyle\int_{\nu_{n}}^{\infty}t^{k}\exp(-\beta t^{2}/2)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\nu_{n}}^{\infty}t\,(t^{2})^{\lceil\frac{k-1}{2}\rceil}\exp(-\beta t^{2}/2)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\frac{\lceil\frac{k-1}{2}\rceil!}{(\beta/4)^{\lceil\frac{k-1}{2}\rceil}}\int_{\nu_{n}}^{\infty}t\,\frac{(\beta t^{2}/4)^{\lceil\frac{k-1}{2}\rceil}}{\lceil\frac{k-1}{2}\rceil!}\exp(-\beta t^{2}/2)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k!}{(\beta/4)^{k/2}}\int_{\nu_{n}}^{\infty}t\Big(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{(\beta t^{2}/4)^{l}}{l!}\Big)\exp(-\beta t^{2}/2)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\frac{k!\,2^{k}}{\beta^{k/2}}\int_{\nu_{n}}^{\infty}t\,\exp(\beta t^{2}/4)\exp(-\beta t^{2}/2)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\frac{k!\,2^{k+1}}{\beta^{(k+2)/2}}\exp(\beta\nu_{n}^{2}/4).

∎

Lemma A.5
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a measurable function with
0\leq f(x)\leq 1
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and let
\mu
be a probability measure on
\mathbb{R}
. Assume that
\mu(\{x:f(x)<1\})>0
. Then

\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu<1.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
we define
A_{n}:=\{x:f(x)<1-1/n\}
, which are increasing for
{n\to\infty}
. By continuity from below,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mu(A_{n})=\mu\big(\{x:f(x)<1\}\big)>0.

Thus, there exists an
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mu(A_{N})>0
and therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu=\int_{A_{N}}f(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu+\int_{A_{N}^{c}}f(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu<(1-\tfrac{1}{N})\mu(A_{N})+\mu(A_{N}^{c})=1-\tfrac{1}{N}\mu(A_{N})<1.

∎

Lemma A.6
.

Let
n\geq 2
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{n}}^{2\sqrt{n}}\big(1+(z-n^{1/2})^{2}\big)^{-a/2}\,\mathrm{d}z\leq\begin{cases}c(0)\sqrt{n},&\text{ if }a=0,\\
c(0)\log n,&\text{ if }a=1,\\
c(0),&\text{ if }a=2.\end{cases}

For
a=0
, the statement is trivial. Otherwise,

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{n}}^{2\sqrt{n}}\big(1+(z-n^{1/2})^{2}\big)^{-a/2}\,\mathrm{d}z=\int_{-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{n}}^{\sqrt{n}}\big(1+z^{2}\big)^{-a/2}\,\mathrm{d}z\leq 2\int_{0}^{\sqrt{n}}\big(1+z^{2}\big)^{-a/2}\,\mathrm{d}z.

Now for
a=1
and the inverse hyperbolic sine
\mathop{\mathrm{arsinh}}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\sqrt{n}}\big(1+z^{2}\big)^{-1/2}\,\mathrm{d}z=\mathop{\mathrm{arsinh}}(\sqrt{n})=\log(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{1+n})\leq c(0)\log(n),

and for
a=2

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\sqrt{n}}\big(1+z^{2}\big)^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}z=\arctan(\sqrt{n})\leq\tfrac{\pi}{2}.

∎

Lemma A.7
.

For
L(x)=x\log x
with
x>0
,

L(1+u+v)=L(1+u)+v+\vartheta_{1}uv+\vartheta_{2}v^{2}

for
|u|\leq 1/4,|v|\leq 1/4
and
|\vartheta_{j}|\leq 2
depending on
u
and
v
.

By Taylor expansion around
v=0
,

\displaystyle(1+u)\log(1+u+v)

\displaystyle=(1+u)\Big(\log(1+u+0)+v(1+u+0)^{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\tfrac{v^{2}}{2}(-1)(1+u+\xi_{1}v)^{-2}\Big)

\displaystyle=(1+u)\log(1+u)+v+v^{2}(1+u)(-\tfrac{1}{2})(1+u+\xi_{1}v)^{-2}

with
\xi_{1}\in[0,1]
depending on
u
and
v
and by Taylor expansion around
u+v=0
,

\displaystyle v\log(1+u+v)

\displaystyle=v\Big(\log(1+0)+(u+v)(1+\xi_{2}(u+v))^{-1}\Big)

\displaystyle=(uv+v^{2})(1+\xi_{2}(u+v))^{-1}

with
\xi_{2}\in[0,1]
depending on
u
and
v
.
Here,

\displaystyle(1+\xi_{2}(u+v))^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq(1-1/2)^{-1}=2,\ \ \ (1+\xi_{2}(u+v))^{-1}\geq(1+1/2)^{-1}=2/3

and

\displaystyle(1+u)\tfrac{1}{2}(1+u+\xi_{1}v)^{-2}

\displaystyle\leq(1+u)\tfrac{1}{2}(1+u-1/4)^{-2}\leq(3/4)\tfrac{1}{2}(1/2)^{-2}=3/2,

\displaystyle(1+u)\tfrac{1}{2}(1+u+\xi_{1}v)^{-2}

\displaystyle\geq(1+u)\tfrac{1}{2}(1+u+1/4)^{-2}\geq(5/4)\tfrac{1}{2}(3/2)^{-2}=5/18.

Consequently,

(1+\xi_{2}(u+v))^{-1}-(1+u)\tfrac{1}{2}(1+u+\xi_{1}v)^{-2}\in[-5/6,31/18]
.

∎

Lemma A.8
.

Let
a,b,l>0
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{-l}^{l}(ax^{2}+b)^{-3/2}\,\mathrm{d}x=2\big(al^{2}+b\big)^{-1/2}\,b^{-1}\,l.

\displaystyle\int_{-l}^{l}(ax^{2}+b)^{-3/2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=2b^{-3/2}\int_{0}^{l}(\tfrac{a}{b}x^{2}+1)^{-3/2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=2b^{-3/2}\sqrt{b/a}\int_{0}^{l\sqrt{a/b}}(x^{2}+1)^{-3/2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=2b^{-3/2}\sqrt{b/a}\,\frac{l\sqrt{a/b}}{\sqrt{l^{2}a/b+1}}=2\big(al^{2}+b\big)^{-1/2}\,b^{-1}\,l.

∎

Lemma A.9
.

Let
a>0
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{-\sqrt{a}}^{\sqrt{a}}(a-w^{2})^{-1/2}\,\mathrm{d}w=\pi.

\displaystyle\int_{-\sqrt{a}}^{\sqrt{a}}(a-w^{2})^{-1/2}\,\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle=2a^{-1/2}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{a}}(1-w^{2}/a)^{-1/2}\,\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle=2a^{-1/2}a^{1/2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-w^{2})^{-1/2}\,\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle=2\arcsin(1)=\pi.

∎
